# What the owner has asked for, by area

Standing rulings, latest words first where they changed. Read the area before changing it, then check the code: a point here
was either built or is still open. Add a ruling here the same day it is given and delete what it overrules (git keeps it; nothing is struck through). Quotes are the
owner's, typos fixed.

## Score editor
- **Recent's keys in their 44 cells (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-19):** on a laptop the docked palette's Recent row drew 48-wide keys 44 apart, each over the next (the harness's OVERLAP, seven times); its keys sit inside their categories' wrappers, out of reach of the grid's `min-w-0` for its direct children. Every key in a grid now narrows to its cell, as the EditorLaptop board's row.
- **The palette's marks in the boards' own serif (type anatomy 7, 2026-11-18):** dynamics, ornaments and articulations (mf, p, tr, >) were set in Instrument Serif, a third face beside Iowan Old Style and Instrument Sans; they keep their engraved bold italic in Iowan now.

- **Loop round 61, the editor boards' menu carries the app's rows (canvas v371):** the four editor boards drew Credits, Instruments, Version history, Layout, MIDI keyboard and Takes, missing Score view, Transpose, Note input, Recording and On-screen piano; they draw the app's three groups now (the score's acts; its settings by name; Export and Print), and the app's MIDI keyboard says the keyboard connected or None, and Export names MusicXML, MIDI, as the boards' values (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, Record a take in the note band (the Editor boards, loop round 46):** it was a row in the ··· menu; it stands between Piano keys and Play, a crimson dot in its ring, on both sizes, and is gone from the menu; a lesson's example has none (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, the score stays under the eye (research F20, checked):** hiding or showing the keys leaves the caret's line where it was mid-score (476 → 476 at 1710, 407 → 407 at 402); only at the score's end, with no room to scroll, does the music settle down (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, no "Use for new songs" in Layout (research F22, not built):** a saved layout default would make Reset mean two things and add a hidden setting; every new song starts from the one layout, and Reset returns to it ("fewer settings") (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, Go to bar (research F11, Flat's and Dorico's ⌘G; canvas v370, EditorLaptop's "Go to bar (⌘G)" state):** the editor had no way to jump to a bar but the arrows; ⌘G hangs a small card under the sheet's head, the caret's bar in its field and "of 16" beside it, Enter takes the caret to that bar's first beat; listed in the shortcuts sheet (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, the editor's menu groups untitled (the boards' MENUCARD; the Player's ruling "i don't like the headings or titles of the settings sections"):** its "Settings" and "Publishing" labels are gone, the groups parted by space; research F21's "This score" and "Editor" titles were not built for the same ruling (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, a slower hand's chord written as one (research F24):** keys struck within 80 ms were one chord, so a slower player's chord split into notes; the window is Dorico's default 100 ms, fixed, with no new setting (fewer settings) (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, Shortcuts from the editor's menu on a laptop (research F23, Flat's rail; canvas v369):** the shortcuts sheet opened only with ?; where keys type them, the menu's settings end with Shortcuts, ? its value, on EditorLaptop and EditorDraftLaptop and in the app (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, no Playback sound row (canvas v368):** the editor boards' menu drew "Playback sound · Grand piano", but the app has one piano sound, so the row could change nothing; it is gone from the four editor boards ("if a control's effect cannot be seen or heard, it is wrong") (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, where the song is kept, under the menu's title (the boards' ED_SAVED):** the learner's own song or version said nowhere that it was saved; the menu says "Saved on this device", or "Saved to your account" when signed in, under the song's name (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, Print from the editor's menu (the boards' Print row):** the editor could not print what it shows; Print, after Export…, prints the draft on A4 pages through the Player's one print, with the editor's chord names, words, fingering, staves in sight and section headings, then asks the browser's print (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, Export from the editor's menu (the boards' Export row):** writing the song out meant leaving for its page; the menu ends with Export… for the learner's own song or version, opening the song page's one export sheet (MusicXML, compressed MusicXML, MIDI) (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, Title and credits heads the editor's menu (the boards' Credits row):** it was reached only from the printed title; it is the menu's first row, the title its value, and its page goes Back to the menu however it was opened (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, the editor's menu titled by the song (Editor, EditorLaptop's `moreOn`):** the ··· sheet read "Score" over the song it acts on; it is titled with the song's name, as the boards draw it, on both sizes (2026-11-17).

The status is the ··· sheet's line under the title, as the editor's "Saved on this device", and the laptop bar's status. The Player boards' icon buttons get their tooltips (Back, Clear, Favourite, More, Share link) from the build's tooltip pass.

- **The editor's save status on both sizes, and the Takes row a value row** (loop round 49, phone and laptop carry the same things): the laptop said "Saved on this device" beside Close and the phone said it nowhere; the phone's bar has no room, so it heads the ··· Score settings sheet, under the title (a status goes in the bar or its menu). "Takes: 3" reads Takes with 3 on the right, as every row there. The laptop's Takes link opened the phone's board; it opens TakesLaptop (Editor, EditorLaptop, EditorDraftLaptop).

- **Loop round 46, the draft's gear opens Score settings (EditorDraft):** an editor's draft of a built-in song drew the editor's ··· but carried none of its sheets, so the gear and its "Score settings open" state did nothing on both sizes; the draft now opens the same Score settings, a sheet on a phone and a popover on a laptop (2026-11-16).

- **Loop round 46, the editor draws its Record (Editor, EditorDraft, Takes):** the record-take feature had no button on any editor board, while Takes' empty state sent the learner to "Record under the score"; Record, a crimson dot in a ring, stands beside Play in the note's band (on a phone over the keys, on a laptop at the palette's head), and Takes says "Press Record beside Play in the editor, play, then Stop" (2026-11-16).

- **Loop round 43, the laptop editor's bar holds no title (EditorLaptop):** "Amazing Grace" beside Close is gone, as round 30 ruled (the score prints it where it is edited); the save status, "Saved on this device", stays beside Close alone (2026-11-16).
- **Loop round 44, every take's Play on its circle (Takes):** on a phone the first take's Play stood bare, its circle turned paper for the laptop's chosen row, which a phone does not draw; the phone's rows keep the grey circle, and only the laptop's chosen row turns it paper on its sand (2026-11-16).
- **Loop round 35, the phone's printed title as the Editor board's:** 21 in the book serif over the lines (it was 24), 30 from a tablet up as the EditorLaptop board's 29 (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 34, the keys under the sheet alone beside the docked panel (EditorLaptop):** on a laptop the keys run from the screen's edge to the panel's hairline, and the panel runs on to the screen's foot, as the board draws them; they no longer pass under the panel (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 31, the tools stay in place, greyed, as Flat's (owner, 2026-11-15: "make the design exactly like the flat editor"; replaces R32-B's "only the tools that act on what is chosen are drawn"):** with no note chosen, Pitch, Articulations, Ornaments, Dynamics and the tempo marks keep every key in their place at 40%, disabled, "Choose a note" their tooltip; the phone's row the same; no line of text stands in their place (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 30, the palette's section titles in the sans** (15, 600, ink), as the Editor boards draw them, no longer the book serif. The laptop bar keeps no title: the score prints it where it is edited, so the board's title beside Close would say it twice.
- **The editor's foot reaches the screen's edge, as Flat's:** what stands last (the keys, else the palette) runs on under the home indicator, its own paper filling the inset, instead of a bare band of page under it.
- **The keys as Flat's (owner, 2026-11-15: "it doesnt make ant sense for the keyboard to close like thet, make like the flat editor does"):** unfolding the palette no longer folds the keys into a "Keyboard" strip; they step aside under the open palette and come back as it folds, their own state untouched; the piano button beside the selection is their one switch, and hidden they leave nothing behind (the strip is gone from the editor).
- **The page view as Flat's (owner, 2026-11-15: "also the page view"):** the pages' canvas reaches the screen's edges with no rounded frame round it; each page's number stands at its head's outer corner, right on an odd page and left on an even one, as Flat and an engraver print it (the Player's pages too).
- **The palette as Flat's (owner, 2026-11-15: "why don't you make the design exactly like the flat editor"; `flat-score-editor` 006–041, `palette-…` and the owner's 17:30 shots):** folded, the chosen category's tools in one row of bare glyphs in soft ink, the chosen on a sand wash; under them, at the sheet's foot, the categories as Flat's tab bar of signs (♩, ♭♯, >, tr, ppp, the barline, 𝄞, ♯♯, 4/4, the repeat, Aa) in a sand pill, each named in its tooltip, and the button that unfolds the palette beside it; unfolded, every section a ruled table of 44px cells in a hairline box, a wide key two cells. The tabs' short names are gone (en, ru). Canvas v204.
- **No fog at a strip's end (owner, 2026-11-15: "and such fog at the end of the stripe for scrolling is not good"):** the palette's category strip and its row of values scroll sideways with no fade at either edge; the next item is simply cut by the edge, as Flat's palette is (`flat-score-editor` 01–03).
- **Loop round 28, the quiet key name by Night:** a plain key's name is the key's own ink at 60%, not the page's soft ink, which vanished on a light key in the dark theme; "C3" reads on every keyboard, Day and Night.
- **Loop round 28, one keyboard as the boards draw it (owner, 2026-11-15: "the keyboard is not consistent across the app with new design"):** a plain key's name ("C4") is 12, medium, in soft ink, a lit or marked key's label bold in its own ink, on every keyboard (Practice, the Player, the editor); a white key is never narrower than the phone boards' 26 (about two octaves across a phone), the editor's keys included, which narrowed to 22 to hold both hands (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 28, the editor's bar as its board (owner, 2026-11-15: "but the headers still dont match"):** no band of paper and no hairline; Close a 44 glass circle; Play · Edit one glass pill of 44 segments, 15px, Edit filled in ink; Undo, Redo and ··· together in one glass pill. The Player's title on a phone takes all the room its buttons leave, so "Words: John Newton · Music: New Britain" shows as the board writes it (2026-11-15).
- **The keys hold middle C when the caret has nothing (loop round 18):** with no note at the caret (a new score, an empty bar, a rest with no hand under it), the editor's keys centre on C3 to C5 as the Editor boards draw them, instead of sitting at the bottom of the score's span; with notes at the caret they still follow them.
- **Review your recording ported to its board** (canvas 195): Quantize's five values in one row (Segmented wraps five only past 20 characters, words, never values), and a flush group pads its switch rows 16 itself, so Hide raw notes and Hear notes no longer touch the card's edge.
- **The takes are drawn** (canvas 194, loop; the Takes board): the list (Play in a quiet circle, the name to its page, length and tempo, ··· with Review, Download, Delete at once with Undo), its empty state, and Review your recording as its page with Back in the header: the take as notes, the roll with its split point, Quantize as one row of five (1/4 1/8 1/16 1/4t 1/8t, never 3 + 2), Hide raw notes, Into ›, then Discard and Save to score. The editor's "Takes: 3" opens it.
- **The recording review's Back is in the sheet's header,** as every sheet's page (loop): the "‹ Back" row in its content and `SheetBack` are gone.
- **The recording review's Discard goes at once, with Undo** (R38, loop): the take is back on the Takes page with "Take deleted · Undo"; "Discard this recording?" is gone.
- **A take's Delete goes at once, with Undo** (R38, loop; the board's "Take deleted · Undo brings it back"): from its row's ··· and its page, "Take deleted · Undo" puts it back in its place; the "Delete this take?" dialog is gone. Toasts now sit above every sheet and dialog (z-60), so an Undo said from the Takes sheet is seen and pressed.
- **Loop round 24, R38 in the editor:** deleting an instrument (or hiding the piano) happens at once, "Deleted
  Voice · Undo" in its toast; Undo steps the score back and restores the staves' view; no question, no "Delete…"
  (2026-11-15).
- **The Editor board brought to the ruled tabs (loop, 2026-11-15, canvas 187):** the board still drew Notes ·
  Accidentals · Articulation · Dynamics · Lines · Bar · Text; it now draws the eleven the app holds, each item where
  `EditorPalette.tsx` keeps it (the slur and fingering in Articulations, the pedal in Dynamics, the octave lines in Pitch).
- **One home each, as Flat (2026-11-05):** "we can at the same time edit the time signature from the settings and from
  the palette which is strange". The time signature, key and clef are set in the palette's category, which their
  printed signs open; the title opens Title and credits; the gear holds only what the score does not print.
- **Only what applies (2026-11-05):** "show only the actions relevant at that time like the write as common in the 5/4
  is not relevant and is weird that it is shown but is disabled".
- **The palette as Flat's, its menus too (2026-11-05):** "this menu in the palette doesn't follow how the flat editor
  does this … as close as possible as the flat editor and its ui and its management and features". Every section in
  one scroll; Time signature as Flat's tiles with Customize; Key signature and Clef bare tables; Tempo in Measure.
- **Professional wording (2026-11-05):** "write professionally, not plain words": precise notation terms and concise
  sentences, as Apple, Flat, MuseScore and Dorico write them, in English and Russian.
- **A real notation app:** "proper scores, proper functions like a real music notation software". Like Flat's: "the same
  features and same music notation and same icons and styles". No hand separation, pattern adding or verse adding inside it.
- **Laptop:** a docked side panel and a note-entry toolbar, never a folded bar at the foot ("annoying and not movable").
  **Phone:** a bottom sheet that shrinks the score, usable as Sibelius, with no page overflow. All staves the same width.
- **Palette:** a real card that keeps its height between tabs and never flickers. Folded, one scrollable row; open, the
  sections as structured, animating well. Tools that only repeat a tap on the sheet go. Navigation and delete leave the note
  tools.
- **Menus:** names fit and say what they do. Bar length and clef change are not "Add". Text terms (dolce) read before
  opening. Real terms: crescendo. Each action its own meaningful icon, never one eye for all. Repeat icons fit.
- **Fingering** is an option where it belongs, never a palette mode.
- **Notation we have, as Flat has:** 64ths, tuplets, octave lines, glissando, every articulation and dynamic, repeats ×N,
  endings 3–4, page breaks, bar repeats, multi-bar rests, tempo changes, Roman numerals, free text, two-note tremolo, grace
  notes of both kinds. Shortcuts in every tooltip; the chord wizard.
- **Editing on the score:** everything printed is editable there. No staff selector. A note chosen, then a value tapped,
  changes that note, and the edge between editing and adding is obvious. Delete goes back note by note. Drag a note up and
  down. Chords typed, or chosen with the app's full chord interface.
- **Staves** are plain: add, remove, rename, hide. Hiding never deletes notes; a toggle never clears the score. A song can
  be a single staff.
- **Every song is editable.** Where something cannot be, the learner is told why.
- **Recording:** the learner chooses what plays along and what shows. The pedal prints one mark per press.
- **The editor's draft (canvas 135, 2026-11-11):** "no roles, no edit mode": a built-in song's original is edited by an editor as a draft under a Publishing pill with Publish…, Versions and Discard changes; what reaches every learner asks first.
- **Loop round 61, Tie writes the next note tied (research F5):** with the caret after a written note or chord, `,` holds it on at the value chosen and moves on, as Dorico's Tie in note input; a note after it of the same key is still joined (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, accidentals reach the note just written (research F6):** with nothing chosen, ♮ ♯ ♭ (0 = −) and Respell change the note or chord the caret last wrote, as Flat's and Dorico's do; no new key (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, chords by interval (research F7):** ⌥2 to ⌥8 add a note a 2nd to an octave above the chord chosen or just written, in the key, ⇧⌥ below, as Flat's; listed in the shortcuts sheet (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, a bar on Cmd (research F10):** ⌘← ⌘→ the previous or next bar and ⇧⌘ extend to it, as Flat and Dorico; Home and End keep the score's ends; Alt with ← → does nothing (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, ⌘A chooses the whole staff (research F12):** Cmd or Ctrl with A selects every note of the staff at the caret, as Flat's and Dorico's, so an accidental, a value or Delete reaches all of it; listed in the shortcuts sheet (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, the bar keys as Flat's (research F14, F16, F18):** ⌥Enter adds a bar after, ⌘⇧Enter one at the end, ⌘Enter the system break, one key pressed where a line starts there and pressed again to join it ("Join lines" is gone); ⌥. the double dot; the shortcuts sheet says the palette's words (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, the palette folds without a jump (research F19):** folding and unfolding cross-fade the tool row and the sections in 150 ms on the app's curve, the sections rising 8; instant under reduced motion (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, Repeat (research F13):** a key after Rest and ⇧R write the notes chosen, else the note or chord just written, again right after them, the copy chosen, as Flat's and Dorico's R; the clipboard is untouched. The canvas's palette now carries the app's keys and shortcuts (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, Voice 2 (research F8):** a key after Chord in Input mode and ⇧V write the piano staff's second voice, stems down under the first, as Flat's and Dorico's V: a held note under a moving tune; each voice's edits leave the other's notes; greyed on a single staff (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, cross-staff (research F9):** Cross to upper staff and Cross to lower staff after the octave pair in Pitch, ⌘⇧↑ ⌘⇧↓, as Dorico's: the notes drawn on the piano's other staff, still their hand's; the key toward their own staff brings them back (2026-11-17).
- **The tempo's pickers say their name once (loop 22, 2026-11-15):** Beat and Tempo text stand at the row's start, so
  their pickers show the value alone (♩, None), in the editor's Tempo sheet and the New song sheet alike.

## Pickers and tempo

- **Time signature:** Current and Pickup columns, numbers stacked large on staff lines as wheels; a cut or common switch; ✗ ✓
  at the bottom right. Every meter, such as 2/2, 3/2, 5/4, 6/4, 7/4, 3/8, 9/8.
- **Key:** a key-signature grid like the music editors', 1–7 sharps over 1–7 flats, the chosen tinted. **No circle of fifths
  as a picker.** C♯/D♭ written as the reference apps write them.
- **Clef:** a bottom sheet with a grid of every clef and its 8va, 8vb, 15ma, 15mb.
- **Tempo:** a large accent circle with BPM and its word, beat units below; hold and swipe to a full-screen ruler, Largo to
  Presto.
- **One control each,** one key picker, one stepper and menu style.
- **The Player's small buttons reach 44 (2026-11-19, the lines; 44px targets):** on the phone and the laptop alike, the Practice sheet's − and + of the tempo and its Tap stood 40, as did a pattern row's heart and Listen and the Pattern sheet's Sort and filter; each is now 44, the size of every other touch target on the phone.

## Player and editor together
- **The Player's Pattern sheet holds the library's shape (the layers walker, 2026-11-17):** its place chips were 34 tall in a row cut at the sheet's edge, and Close all a 28-tall link in an orange at 3.9:1. The chips reach 44 as the kit's and wrap in the sheet (nothing is cut in a sheet); Close all reaches 44 in the darker orange (#A8551F, as Continue's label).
- **The score's systems keep Verovio's spacing (port P4, 2026-11-18):** the board measures 24 between its drawn systems' boxes, chord symbols and bar numbers included; in the app the gap is the engraving's own (`spacingSystem`, the learner's Space between systems on a page), set by ink and not by the 4/8 scale. Nothing is forced to 24: an engraver's spacing outranks a screen's grid (LilyPond, Verovio).
- **The Player's two saved boards brought level (2026-11-18):** PlayerApp and PlayerDesk come from the published copy, not a generator, and still drew the Pattern sheet's chips 34 tall in a cut row, Close all 28 tall at 3.9:1 and each pattern's hands on one cut line; their chips now reach 44 on the kit chip's 4px transparent edges and wrap, Close all reaches 44 in #A8551F, and the hands line takes two lines (lines tool: 0 problems at 402 and 1710). Their favourite hearts were honey-deep where every library board's heart is the red #B84A3E (rest #B4AAA0); they are the library's now (similarity: one heart). Ported (E1): the app's Pattern sheet is the library's shape, one implementation with Accompaniment (`useLibraryArrangement`, `libraryShown`); Texture and Feel filters wait for the patterns to carry that data.
- **The next lyric line readable (loop 63, 2026-11-17, canvas v460):** under the Diagrams view's current line the next one was #A39A90 on the paper, 2.5:1; it is soft ink now (#685E56, 4.5:1 and over), its lighter weight keeping it second (contrast, WCAG 1.4.3). `PlayerApp`, `PlayerDesk`.

- **Loop round 61, a laptop's settings are a card under their button (EditorLaptop's and PlayerDesk's popover):** the editor's gear and the Player's Settings opened as a full-height panel at the right, which covered the editor's Undo, Redo and ···, cut its layout pill, and squeezed the Player's music; both are now the board's card, 400 wide, 16 round with the hairline and the second shade, hung 8 under the bar's buttons at their right edge, fading in place, the music keeping its width, a click outside closing it (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 32, a key down and the chosen note's keys in sky:** the boards' piano paints a key down `#85B8DC` (`--key-down`), the score's selection colour; the app had painted it and the editor's chosen keys honey, the one action's colour. Every keyboard now (2026-11-15). The editor's credits line reaches 44 under the title (it was 38).
- **Loop round 32, the Keyboard button wears one glyph:** the keys, whatever the place shows; its caption says 3D, 2D or Off (it had swapped to a hand or a crossed eye). The Player boards drew a grand piano there and now draw the keys too, the one piano glyph (2026-11-15).
- **One score in two modes:** "I don't like how the header looks with this bar under the header". One designed header, the
  same transport at the foot, Edit straight from the Player, nothing jumping.
- **One gear per screen** (Flat's), Edit beside the title,. Settings never scattered over four menus.
- **Transpose** works exactly the same in both: semitones, the key it lands in, Apply to key or chords, a preview. No circle
  of fifths.
- **Floating controls** hide when the learner taps elsewhere, reliably. Nothing traps a click.

- **One heading on the score in Play and in Edit (loop 63, 2026-11-17):** the editor printed the song's heading its own way (a 22 title, the credits pushed right on the verse's line); it now prints the Player's, value for value: the title centred (24 on a phone, 36 on a laptop), the credits centred under it, then the verse and tempo at the score's left 16 (24) below, the title at the same height in both modes, so switching Play and Edit changes the tools, not the page (consistency, Jakob; the flow lines). Only the laptop editor's docked palette moves the column, as ruled (canvas v421).

## Sheet, selection and playhead

- **Names:** Page view, Vertical scroll, Panorama.
- **Quality:** always sharp at every zoom. The page view looks printed: margins, line breaks, chord sizes. Study real scores,
  LilyPond and abcjs. Verovio, the owner's choice.
- **The page canvas** floats in infinite space, dragged and pinch-zoomed. Vertical scroll takes the full width, every line
  equal.
- **Selection:** tight to the notes, padded from neighbours, the same everywhere; one note of a chord can be chosen; a range
  is exactly the bars chosen; it works from the keyboard. A note clicked in the Player jumps to that note.
- **Two marks:** what is chosen and what is playing are separate; playing is one line across the staves in sight.
- **The editor's selection tight to the note (loop round 58, held against Dorico `001`–`005` and Flat):** the chosen note wore a sky column from over its chord symbol to under the staff, picking the chord and empty staff with it; it now wraps the note's head and stem alone, as Dorico and Flat mark the note itself, on the editor's four boards (2026-11-16).
- **A score's words never touch (loop round 58, engraving):** on a phone the song's page set "zing grace," and "that saved" against each other, the notes spaced by duration alone; each note now advances at least half of its word and the next one's plus a gap, the rest of the line shared by duration, as an engraver widens a bar for its lyrics; wherever the melody is drawn (2026-11-16).
- **A chord after a line's last word stays on the chord row (loop round 58, the Player's Lyrics):** D⁷ after "like me!" and G after "I see." dropped onto the words' line, their empty word giving the column no height; each word now keeps its line's height, so every chord stands over the line, phone and laptop (2026-11-16).
- **Chord names** are not cluttered or too big; a long chord never breaks the layout.

## Player views and settings
- **Lyrics' pinned diagrams as the board's row (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-19):** each chord's framed keys 42 tall, its name 8 under them in the sans at 500, 14, in ink with its extensions raised, the chords 16 apart, the one sounding named bold; they were 32 tall keys in a box outlined while sounding, the names in the serif, the others muted.
- **Lyrics as PlayerApp's Lyrics view draws it (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-19):** a chord over the words is a chip in the sans at 700, 13 to the words' 16, 21 tall, 5 round, 7 in, its extensions raised (G⁷), 2 over the words; the line playing bold and the others in ink at 400 (they were muted), lines 8 apart; a verse's head in the sans caps 12 at 700, 0.04em, 8 over its first line and 24 under the last verse (it was the serif spaced 0.3em). They were serif chips at 600 with a 2em least width.
- **A view's settings as rows in one card, as PlayerViewSheet, PlayerViewDiagrams, PlayerViewLyrics and PlayerViewGrid draw them (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-19):** each view's part of Score view is one white card of 48 rows on the sheet, a choice's name at the left and its control at the row's end (Layout, Mode, Bars per row, Text size), each part it shows a switch row (R43: an on or off is a switch row, never a chip); the sheet music's Show is its own card under its caption. They were chips under a caps caption, Mode and Bars per row stacked under their names, Text size with an Aa tile. The editor's Score view shows the same rows.
- **The Grid's Bars per row, as its boards (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-19):** PlayerViewGrid's Grid settings hold Bars per row, 2 · 4; the board lays two on a phone and four on PlayerDesk. The app laid as many as the width held (ten on a laptop). Now the Grid lays the chosen number, or two on a phone held upright and four from 640px until one is chosen (`scoreView.gridBars`, its home the Player). On a laptop the Grid stands 120 in from the window's sides, its cells 66 tall with the chord 28 and 14 in, the head 32 in, the numbers' gutter 26, as PlayerDesk measures.
- **The Grid's rows as PlayerViewGrid draws them (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-19):** a section's head in the sans caps, 12 at 700, soft, 22 in and 14 under the credits (later ones 24 under the last row), its bar range gone; each row's first bar number in a 16 gutter, 10 and faint, 8 before the cells; every beat a white cell 44 tall, 3 round, 1 apart, a bar opening on a 3 ink rule, its chord 8 in and centred; rows 4 apart. It was a ruled 48 band per row with the number in each bar's corner, the head 20 in the serif with "bars 1–16". A song's chart keeps its own shape.
- **The Grid's chords in its board's face (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-19):** PlayerViewGrid sets a chord in the sans at 500, 19 at most, its seventh raised (G⁷); the Explore board's song chart in the book serif at 700, 22. The one `CellChords` takes its chart's face: the Player's Grid (a bar that takes the Player there) the sans, a song's chart and its print the serif; both raise the extensions at max(12px, .6em). It was the serif at 600, 20, unraised, everywhere.
- **A phone on its side keeps 44 targets (loop, the 852×393 check, 2026-11-19):** the transport's buttons and the editor's palette keys and selection acts shrank to 40 on a phone on its side; the transport is one row there and fits its 48, and the palette's keys fit 44, so neither shrinks now (Fitts; 44 on touch).
- **The laptop's full screen as PlayerDesk and EditorLaptop draw it (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-19):** the Player and the editor stood in the 56 gutter kept beside a sidebar, their bar 24 under the top; with no sidebar the boards put the bar 14 under the top and 24 in from each side, and the transport 30 above the foot (it was 14). The title stands 19 under the bar's buttons on a phone and 32 from 640px, its credits 8 under it at 13 (15 from 640px; they were 4 under it at 13, a page's print gap). The views pill is Play · Edit's shape: 44 segments at 15, 18 a side, at least 76 (they were 112 × 34 at 14 in a padded pill), so `ViewPill` has one size. `FullScreenLayout`, `LINES_HEAD`, `SheetCredits`, `ViewPill`.
- **The score's title as its boards print it (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-19):** PlayerApp and Editor print the title 24 at 1.1, EditorLaptop and PlayerDesk 36 at 1.1; the app's was 22 and 34 at the theme's leading. `SheetTitle`'s lines place is 24 and 36 at 1.1 now, Play and Edit alike, and the editor's title keeps its 44 reach (20 above the shorter line).
- **A scrolling keyboard opens on a whole key (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-19):** a range wider than the view opened wherever the lit notes put it, so the editor's keys began on part of a white key at the left; the Editor board starts on a whole C3. Now the opening scroll lands on the nearest white key's edge (`onWhiteEdge`), everywhere the piano scrolls.
- **No coach tip over the Player's music (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-19):** a first visit floated "Tap a note to start from there, then press Play." over the second system, covering its notes; no Player board draws it and no ruling keeps it, so it went with its remembered `tipsSeen` (the settings store's version bumped). The transport and the score say what to do.
- **The view capsule's segments reach 44 on touch (loop, tablet widths, 2026-11-19):** Sheet · Diagrams · Grid · Lyrics are 34 tall inside the 44 glass capsule, and on an iPad the 5 above and below answered nothing; a coarse pointer's reach now fills the capsule (`ViewPill`'s `before`), the drawing unchanged.
- **The Player's small type on the scale (type anatomy 1 and 10, the lines sweep, 2026-11-18):** the transport's labels under their icons, the sheets' caps heads (Sort by, Level, Each bar, Tempo), the beat numbers and the pattern badge were 11px and are 12; caps heads tracked at 0.08, 0.16 and 0.2em are all 0.04em; chord superscripts are max(12px, .6em); the lyrics put a chord 2 over its word, lines 8 apart by the ink and verses 24, the chips 8 apart.
- **A laptop popover's title stands on its cards' edge (loop round 67; the lines, measured by ink: a 4px near-miss):** Parts, Piano, Practice and the view's popover (Sheet, Diagrams, Grid, Lyrics) set their title with `margin: 0 4px`, 4 in from the grouped cards under it, while every other popover title (Transpose, Pattern, Sort and filter) stands on the cards' edge. Now all of them do. `player/r43_player.py` and the built PlayerDesk.
- **The grid's beats join, as Chordify's (laws, A phone is not a small laptop 4, 2026-11-18; `chordify/004-player-grid-view-chord-table.png`):** the Grid view's beat cells stood 4 apart, neither 8 nor joined; Chordify keeps them about 1pt apart so a row reads as one ruled bar with its barline. They stand 1 apart now, phone and laptop; the barline before each bar is unchanged. The lines tool no longer counts a float over content as a target's neighbour.
- **Played through floats on a laptop, as PlayerDesk draws it (loop round 60):** a 440 card 112 above the window's foot, clear of the transport, 16 round with its soft shade, where the app pinned a 670 sheet to the foot; the phone keeps its sheet. One sheet, a `floating` choice (2026-11-17).
- **A Wait pass counts its pauses (loop round 62, research item 14, Chordio's time per step):** a step that takes over two beats at the chosen tempo is a pause for its bar; Played through says it under the four bars' slips ("No slips · 2 pauses", soft ink, only when there are some), the score never counts it, and of two equally weak pairs of bars the one with more pauses is the one to practise (canvas v376) (2026-11-17).
- **Loop round 61, Played through ported from its board (PlayerDone):** a Wait pass at the song's end opens the sheet: the score and the best, the tempo and its word, the bars four at a time painted by their slips (each a button that loops its four, the weakest ringed), Practise bars n–m at 70 %, Play again, Try it in Play, the days in a row; Wait mode's line keeps only the score, its Again and Loop gone to the sheet (2026-11-17).
- **The Player's group labels in the caps (loop round 60, 2026-11-16):** the Practice sheet's "Tempo" and "Loop bars 5–8" had lost the caps that rounds 15 and 25 give every group label; they wear the kit's CAPS again (13 semibold, 4% tracking), on phone and laptop.
- **Loop round 50, a hand's volume reads quiet to loud (Parts):** the speaker at the slider's start followed the level, so at 80% both ends wore the same two-wave speaker; it is the one-wave speaker at every level now (still the muted mark while muted), the two-wave one stays at the loud end, on both sizes (2026-11-16).
- **Loop round 50, Transpose chooses a key as every key picker does (PlayerKey):** the chosen letter wore the honey wash, the one action's colour; it is the sand fill with no ring, as the Chords page's and every key picker's, on both sizes and by Night (2026-11-16).
- **Loop round 46, the laptop's keyboard button says what the keys show (PlayerDesk):** it read "Piano" while the phone's reads 3D, 2D or Off under the same keys glyph (loop round 32's ruling); the laptop now says 3D, 2D or Off too (2026-11-16).

- **Loop round 45, a view starts under the bar with the keys shown (PlayerViewLyrics, PlayerViewGrid, every Player board with keys):** with the keys on, Lyrics and the Grid started 16 from the screen's top, from before the bar was a band, so the beat strip and Verse 1 ran under the status bar and the bar; they start under the bar now (a phone 128, a laptop 80), as without the keys (2026-11-16).
- **Loop round 39, Parts' hand tiles as the board's PART:** 40 square at 4 corners, the hand 24 in its deep paint (brick #9A4B3A, lilac #6A5988) on its wash, by Day and by Night; it was 10 corners and the light paint, faint on the wash by Night (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 39, the gear's Share link and ··· as the PlayerApp board:** white circles with a 6% ring and a 1px shade (`0 0 0 1px`, `0 1px 2px` at 8%), not the floating elevation (12% and a 3px 8px shade); by Night the hairline alone (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 35, Arrangement or Accompaniment (owner, 2026-11-07; the PlayerApp board):** the gear's head is two radio cards, each holding its own choice in 48-tall rows: Arrangement, the song's own pattern, with its Level; Accompaniment, played from the chords by a pattern you choose, its Pattern › opening the Accompaniment page (the pattern and its hands, Inversion, Swing), no longer a row of its own and no longer heading itself twice. Choosing Arrangement or a level brings the song's own pattern back; choosing Accompaniment opens its page (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 35, the gear's Arrangement card (owner, 2026-11-07; the PlayerApp board):** what you play heads the Player's Settings under the song: the Arrangement card, chosen, its Level row under it, Easy · Simplified · Original, easiest first (Simplified greyed with its reason where it changes no chord), and a Scoring row (your own version, the piano alone, with the voice) where the song has more than one; the Version page and its row are gone. A radio card without a picture takes no tile (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 34, no double gap in Player settings:** an empty column for the screen's own choices stood between the song's head and the first group, so they stood 41 apart; empty, it now takes no room, and they stand the sheet's 20 apart (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 34, Play wears the board's metronome (`METRO`):** the Practice button and its Play card draw the board's case, pendulum and bar (`PlayAlongIcon`), not Lucide's metronome; Wait keeps the hourglass (2026-11-15).
- **The keys button says what the keys show, phone and laptop (loop round 46, 61):** 3D, 2D or Off under the keys glyph at every size, as the PlayerDesk board's `kbCap`; the laptop's "Piano" is gone (2026-11-16).
- **Loop round 34, the laptop's transport names the hands in full:** Both hands, Right hand, Left hand (Обе руки…) beside the picture, as the PlayerDesk board; a phone keeps Both under it (`captionWide` on the bar's button) (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 33, the Player's bar band as its board:** 12 over the buttons and 8 under them, a 64 band under the status bar (it was 8 and 4, 56); the editor's floating bar keeps no band (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 29, the laptop's views pill as the PlayerDesk board:** a 44 glass pill padded 4 holding four 112 × 34 segments at 14, 600 (Sheet · Diagrams · Grid · Lyrics), the shown filled in ink; the one pill (`ViewPill`) takes a size, the editor's Play · Edit keeping its 44 segments.
- **Loop round 25, the Practice sheet's Tempo heading (PlayerApp):** the tempo and the metronome sit under "Tempo", the group's caps label, as the board heads them; the tempo face stays the Metronome's one dial (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 25, radio cards as the boards' (PlayerApp's mode tiles, CARDV):** the chosen card in a honey wash ringed 2px in the deep honey, its dot honey with a white tick, the others white with a ringed dot; Wait · Play stand side by side, 14 round with 44 by 40 tiles, and cards holding rows (the 3D piano) stack, one `RadioCards` (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 25, the Settings' head as the board's (PlayerApp):** the song's tile with its two notes, its title 17 bold over "G major · 3/4 · 80 BPM" in 13, Share link and ··· white circles lifted a little, Edit score 44 tall with 12 corners in 15 semibold (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 25, the transport's groups as the board measures them (PlayerApp, PlayerDesk):** three glass groups 56 tall, 4 inside, 8 apart, every button 48 tall (‹ › 44 wide, Play 48) on a phone from 390px; on a laptop each picture stands beside its word in 15px semibold, 12 before and 16 after (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 25, the transport floats over the music (the board's PlayerApp):** its three glass groups stand over the music's foot with no band or hairline under them, the music running on beneath to the screen's edge and scrolling clear of the keys' window, else of the transport; the R31-P row at the foot is gone (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 24, the title in the bar, no floating circles (owner, IMG_6045–6056: "the titles doesnt look good and should be in the headers ... no settings in the view buttons. floating buttons with backroung doesnt make sense"; canvas v201):** the Player's bar is a band of paper with its hairline: Close and the gear plain icons, the song's title (the page's heading) and who wrote it beside Close; the views a pill in the middle from 640px, on a phone one button wearing the view shown that opens the four as a menu. A view button only shows its view. No view prints the title any more (lines, across, pages, Grid, Diagrams, Lyrics); a printed score keeps its own (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 24, the loop's grips drag across lines (owner, IMG_6045–6056: "the drag holders to loop doesnt work"):** a grip was held to its own line, and a phone lays one or two bars to a line, so it could not move; now each line marks its bars, a grip follows the finger over the whole score (the window keeps the drag when the grip moves to another line), the start from the first bar to the loop's end and the end from its start to the piece's last bar, and the click a drag ends with is swallowed. The score's scroller keeps 12px each side so the end grip's tab is never cut (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 24, the piano a window over the music (owner, IMG_6045–6056: "piano should also be floating and draggabble and resizeble from all the angles"; canvas v202):** the keys, 3D or 2D, are a rounded, lifted window over the music's foot above the transport (the phone's 8px from each side, the laptop's 1100 wide and centred, as the board draws them), never a docked row. Its grip along the top moves it (the arrow keys too; a double tap puts it back); a handle at each corner and on the left, right and bottom sizes it. Each screen size keeps its own window; the music scrolls clear of it and the first tip sits above it. The keys' dock setting no longer acts in the Player; the divider is gone (2026-11-15).
- **Loop round 24, the Parts sheet (owner, IMG_6045–6056: "hands settings should be refactored"; the board's Parts, R43 canvas 119):** the transport's hands open Parts: a card for each hand, the right first, with its picture in its paint, its name and a switch for whether you play it (one stays on), its volume (how loud the app plays it when you don't; 0, not at all) and, on the hand you don't play, Dim. Left · Both · Right, Hear the other hand, Player settings › Hands and the other hand's Hide are gone: the volume and Dim say it all, in one home (2026-11-15).
- **No pedal under silence (loop round 18):** music with no chord symbols and no pedal marks is pedalled bar by bar only where a bar sounds a note; a new song's empty bars, or a bar of rests, carry no Ped. and no pedal is held.
- **Loop round 24, no explanation under a pattern:** the Accompaniment page's Pattern row is its name alone (a
  mix still says "Left hand changed"); the Pattern list shows each pattern's two hands as bars, as the hands' lists
  do, with a line only for why one cannot be chosen ("Needs a melody staff"); what a pattern is stays in Pattern's ⓘ
  (2026-11-15).

- **Loop round 19, the Player's Piano sheet to its board (r43, `KBVALS`):** the 3D card holds Angle (Seat · Above) and Hands on the keys, and Range follows the cards: Fit the song · 61 keys · 76 keys · 88 keys (C2–C7, E1–G7, A0–C8), each kept in the Player's own settings. Every segmented control is the board's SEG: each segment as wide as its words, never wrapped, the room left shared (2026-11-15).

- **Loop round 17, nothing under the bar:** the progress line along the score's head is gone, as the Player's boards draw none; the bar stands bare over the paper (2026-11-15).

- **Loop round 15, chord symbols raised as the boards set them:** over the staff, in the Player and the editor, and in the engraving, a symbol's numbers and alterations are raised (G⁷, C♯m⁷♭⁵) and its accidentals printed as signs, one splitter (`symbolParts`) shared with the symbols in a line (`ChordText`) (2026-11-14).
- **Loop round 14, who wrote it under the title, as the boards print it:** one quiet line, "Words: John Newton · Music: New Britain" in 13px, centred under the Player's title and at the right of the editor's and a printed page; a built-in song's credits fill the score's lines where it has none, so every song prints them (2026-11-14).

- **Layout's one home in the Player is its view's settings (loop round 11, the Player board's view sheet):** the
  board draws Layout as the Sheet section's first row, before Show; the floating pill over the score is gone from the
  Player. The three layouts stay (Songscription's Endless tall). The editor keeps its gear's View on a phone and the
  pill beside its docked panel.
- **The tip that teaches (loop, 2026-11-13):** the first tip says what a
  learner cannot guess ("Tap a note to start from there, then press Play"), never what the Play button already says.
- **Songscription's score page is a Player reference (2026-11-11):** "will this help you", "ok document this": the owner's saved copy, read for its controls, is written up in `.scratch/r39/plan/player-reference-songscription.md` with five proposals for the Player canvas (Endless tall, tempo by dragging, the song's keys only, shortcuts beside their names, Reset in the Key sheet). The export itself never enters the repo: it shows the owner's account and carries a recorded song.
- **Musicca's chord player page, read (2026-11-11):** the owner's export of musicca.com/chord-player is compared with our Chord player in `.scratch/r39/plan/practice-chord-player.md` (Reference): Variation per section, Time signature, Download as audio, beat ticks and edge-dragged lengths, typed chords, draggable chords. Exports stay out of the repo.
- **A sheet's height is checked, not assumed; a tiny target the tap ignores becomes the real target (R43, canvas
  122):** "the piano settings in the player are not correct height and the rows don't have enough place". Rows shrank
  in a flex sheet and the sheet ran under the bar. Every sheet's children are `flex: none`; a phone sheet stops below
  the bar. Played through's 18-wide bars practised four at a time anyway, so the four became the button. The Chord
  player got the one Piano sheet and the same live 3D code, and the Player got Night through the Chords page's map.
- **One thing, one markup, imported by every page that has it (R43, canvas 121):** "merge them and make proper one
  piano settings". The Player's and the Chords page's piano sheets had drifted (Angle on one, Hands on the keys and
  the Range strip on the other). Now `piano_sheet.py` draws the one Piano sheet for both; only the choices follow
  the page (a chord's octaves, a song's own piano).
- **Measure before saying a turn is done (R43, canvas 120):** "did you check the paddings, the spacing and all other?".
  A screenshot is not a check. Each publish runs `spacing_measure.js`, `layout_check.js` and the hit-area count on every
  board the turn touched, and the report quotes the numbers.
- **A menu is named by what it holds, from the references, not the owner's draft word (R43, canvas 119):** "research
  and what professional terms should we use and dont take my advice as such". Hands became **Parts** (Yamaha,
  Synthesia, Flat). The rows are Right hand and Left hand, not "Piano left hand", and the hand's picture is the same in
  the bar and the menu. Honey chips for on/off became switch rows.
- **One icon, one meaning, defined once (R43, canvas 118):** "why the icons of the play mode differ in the drawer and
  in the toolbar". A mode's picture is one map read by every place that shows the mode; Play is a metronome, Wait an
  hourglass, everywhere.
- **A style carried over means every sheet and control, not the bar alone (R43, canvas 117):** "but you didnt do the
  proper refactoring of the settings of the buttons of the styles and so on". The Player's sheets now use the Chords
  kit throughout: centred title and Done, green switches, plain groups, radio cards with a check, Keyboard as Chords'.
- **The Player speaks the Chords page's language (R43, canvas 116):** "update the player with the same style updates
  and instructions". The transport as three glass groups, ‹ ▶ › the tinted one, each symbol over its
  value on a phone; Transpose the Chords page's Key (letters, ♭ ♮ ♯ on the chosen letter, spelled by direction, Back
  to the written key). A pattern settled on one page is carried to every page that has the same thing.
- **The end of a run says what happened and what next** (2026-11-07, the redesign skill's evidence of care): Played
  through, the score with its comparison, each bar's slips, Practise bars n–m, Play again, the next step. Drawn (v149).
  At the end of the song only, not after a loop ("song only").
- **No Song information and no Song page row in the Player** ("no song information", "no song page in the settings"). Built in loop round 1 (2026-11-13), with My recordings, How did I play, Voice commands, the Rhythm game and
  the Tuner gone from the Player; the recording's Tuning is a chip on the song page's fact line.
- **A settings drawer holds settings; its acts sit in its head** ("the first two three settings should be more
  distinctive", "feels like a bottom bar and not like settings drawer", "still not good. research more"): the song at
  the drawer's head with Share link and ··· (Print · Export), as Chordify's ⋮; settings under headers below.
  Never a row of icon buttons in a drawer, never acts listed among settings.
  Edit score takes the whole row under the song's name, Share link and ··· beside the name, never Edit inside ···
  ("the edit button is too hidden", "the edit button takes the whole row"); no section
  headings over the drawer's groups ("i don't like the headings or titles of the settings sections").
- **Settings a learner understands, ordered by what matters to them;** the sheet's View settings usable; nothing reserves
  a band above the keyboard, nor a tip; proper pedals, not a lever at the keys' corner (piano sites as the reference);
  the Player smooth with many notes (the Fantaisie hung). Build the approved mockup whole, "not only half" (2026-11-05).
  `/settings/player` gone, the gear the Player's settings' home.
- **Proper pianos, proper pedals (2026-11-05):** "i need proper pianos with proper pedal and implementations" (musicca,
  piano.org). Built in R33: ivory and ebony keys with felt and depth everywhere; the sustain held, latched (a lit
  indicator) or the score's, every key under it released together with a real damper; soft quieter and darker,
  sostenuto as a piano's; the Player's
  piano settings stay in its sheet. Record and Mark not repeated on the keys (the editor's Record, the Chord finder).
- **Songscription's keyboard (2026-11-05):** "it doesn't still look good with these, and this rail at the bottom, look
  at the songscription piano implementation"; "i don't want finger number under the keyboard and no piano roll yet".
  Built in R34: the rail gone, the keys slim and edge to edge, the chord held; no piano roll.

- **The Player's Pace and metronome (2026-11-07, canvas R37):**
  - Modes are Wait and Play only, no Follow, no group titles.
  - Sheet settings have no Your marks switch: the learner's own marks always show, and are removed by deleting them.
  - The transport's menu is Practice, never Pace. It holds Wait · Play, the metronome, and the loop. A song's recording has no settings anywhere in the Player: it
    plays along, and a wrong start is fixed in the content.
  - The Song gear has two radio cards: Arrangement (its Level) and Accompaniment
    (Pattern ›). Versions and accompaniment are separate, one plays. Rejected: "As written", a switch over version
    tiles, one mixed list. Names are the trade's: flowkey's levels, Smart Pianist's Accompaniment and Pattern. No Voicing or Swing; the accompaniment is not in
    Practice.
  - The Player's Pattern page is a library: Search; place chips at the top (All, Favourites, Recently used, each
    book, Mine; the owner likes them); a Sort and filter page (Sort by, Level, Texture, Feel,
    Reset · Show n) with its count on the button and removable chips; folders that fold by their header (Recently
    used and Favourites open, each book closed, its groups inside, Mine's own folders), Close all / Open all; rows with roll, hands line, level, Listen, heart. Never chips by
    kind, stars or cards two a row.
  - The Player keeps Sections, Print and Share link. No My recordings, no How did I play, no Voice commands, anywhere; no Tuner in the Player.
  - The metronome is Flat's: one Off · Count-in · On choice under the tempo, and Metronome sound › opens a page
    (sound list with a check, Volume, Back). A choice with its own list opens a page, as Flat's Playback options do.
  - No cursor or scrolling settings at all, neither a switch nor choices (rejected twice). The cursor moves by
    note and the score scrolls when needed; a scroll by hand shows a Back to the music pill.
  - No ask-on-open, no skin tone (one neutral hand), no resume-following setting.
  - The tempo belongs to the metronome, in BPM with − + Tap and its marking, Score tempo to go back.
  - One setting never disables another.
  - The meter and accents come from the score: no beat squares, no subdivision in the Player.
  - Sounds named for what they are, and every one plays.
  - References are sheet-music players, never recording software.
  - No pedal setting: "remove it". Playback pedals as marked or automatically.
  - The sheet's Show chips are the app's names. No cursor setting.
  - The full list is in `.scratch/r37/plan/pace-and-settings.md`, "The rulings as they stand".
- Every Chordify feature but guitar. Plain which view a setting belongs to; Layout is the sheet music's alone.
- **Diagrams** animate; strip and diagrams swipe by themselves, the page never moves; no odd scroll bar; a swipe goes to the
  next chord.
- **Taps on chords** move the player there and open no dialog.
- **Diagrams as its board draws it (owner, 2026-11-16: "why did you wrongly refactored and implemented the flow and the functions of the diagram view of the player"; loop round 61):** the app had kept its older pair of two fixed slots and a strip that turned pages, and its unframed diagram, instead of porting the board's code. Now: a row of every change, the one sounding at the start and the rest at 38%, swiped a chord at a time (the music follows) or tapped; the beat strip one row of 36px cells (52 on a laptop) following the beat a third of the way in; the line sung under it; the title's credits under the title; one framed chord tile app-wide.
- **Settings:** a song with written staves plays its staves; patterns, inversion and voice leading belong to a chord chart
  alone; "Melody" goes. A section can have its own pattern. Old settings nobody needs go.
- **Drum grooves:** Chordify's.
- **The Practice sheet's tempo and metronome are one group (loop 22, 2026-11-15):** under Wait · Play, one white card,
  the tempo's steps with BPM over them, then Metronome Off · Count-in · On, a hairline
  between, as Scale settings' rows; the steps tighten to fit a 393 phone.

## Learn and the editors
- **Learn and Profile start where their boards do (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-19):** every place board starts its content 8 under the head's 56 band on a phone (the head's 4 pad and the main's 4) and flush under the 88 head on a laptop; Songs did, but Learn started 4 under it (16 on a laptop: 76 against 88) and Profile 4 (96 on a laptop). Both now stand at 126 and 88, as Songs. A laptop level, course or own row is the board's 64 content and 10 pads, 84 tall where the app's were 80, so the Courses head and its cards stood 4 high.
- **A level's lessons wrap their titles at 1024 (loop, tablet widths, 2026-11-19):** in two columns of 314 a lesson's title would not shrink, so it ran under Next and the next column ("Half steps, whole steps, sharps and…" under Lesson 7); the title now wraps in its cell (`LessonRow`).
- **Made by you's rows one height (the lines, similarity, 2026-11-18):** on a phone a course's row stood 76 and every lesson's 72 in the one list, the course's min-height 64 where the lesson's is 60; both are now 60 with the same 6 padding, so every row is 72.

- **Lessons, courses and levels, and Learn's structure (2026-11-18, "what is the difference between them … how can we improve this learn page structure. research more and check the examples"; canvas v547):** a lesson is one page of sections learned in one sitting; a level is the main path, its Journey of lessons in order; a course is a short focused topic (reading, rhythm, the pedal) taken beside the path, with its own progress. The examples share one order, and Learn keeps it: Continue first (flowkey's Continue Learning), then the path (Duolingo's sections, the journey app's "0/20 complete"), then the courses on a shelf (flowkey's Courses). What changed: every course card's eyebrow repeated its level in capitals ("BEGINNER" on four of five, which tells the learner nothing); it now says what they hunt for, the card's progress ("3 of 8 lessons", "8 lessons", "Done"), in sentence case (data drives the form; scan-first 7). Examples read: `piano-data/reference-apps/flowkey` 001–003, `duolingo-music` 001, `courses-app/001-home`, `journey-app/001-beginner-journey-foundations`.
- **Course cards on a laptop carry no chevron (2026-11-17):** the Courses page's laptop cards kept a chevron at their right end while Learn's level and own-lesson cards had dropped theirs: a card is tapped whole and ends where its words end. Gone, so the two pages draw a course card one way.
- **Continue reads as one row on a laptop (2026-11-17):** the laptop's Continue card held its words at the left and its button 1,000px away at the right, a row cut in two (proximity). The level's progress track now fills the space between them, its "Lesson 7 of 13" above its right end (goal-gradient), and the line under the title that said the same goes. The phone keeps its card as it was: the button is beside the words there.
- **The lesson's icon is Lucide's book (2026-11-17):** the lesson glyph was a hand-made book with no spine curl, read as a square (a Stop) on Continue's dark button. It is Lucide's `book` wherever a lesson appears (Continue, the lesson rows, Your lessons, Search), and Settings' User guide, which wore the same glyph, takes `circle-help`: one icon, one meaning.
- **A lesson's In short under its example (owner, 2026-11-17, the yellow boxes circled at the right edge: "i dont like this alignment of these yellow explanations on the right side"):** on a laptop In short stood in a 380 column at the right, pushed to the words' foot, apart from what it sums up; it now follows the section's example on the column's left edge, sized to its words (65ch at most), in the phone's order: words, example, In short (proximity; one edge).
- **Learn and a level on one laptop screen, no rows cut in two (the lines' sweep, 2026-11-17):** Learn's Levels and Your lessons and courses stood side by side as 660-wide lists, the count and chevron 400 from their words and an 80 hole under the shorter list; now Levels are four cards in a row, then Courses, then the learner's own as three cards, the phone's order, each card ending where its words end. A level's page starts on its title's edge (it was a centred 880 column 195 in from the title), its head as wide as a line of reading, each chapter's lessons in a row of three with no spine or far chevron, Next beside the title, the chapter's count beside its name. "Across the app" draws Learn from the same parts.
- **A take's labels as every field's (loop 63, 2026-11-17, canvas v459):** a take's Name was a small grey word on the field's edge while Keep bars wore the soft caps 16 in; Name wears the caps 16 in, the field's words start 16 in under it, and Delete stands 24 apart from Keep bars, phone and laptop (similarity; destructive acts apart). `Take`, `TakeLaptop`.

Select in the bar (Cancel while on, the title "n selected"), a checkbox leading every row in one column (a course's lessons keep their indent after the box), the row's ··· and the + gone meanwhile, the floating glass bar with Duplicate and Delete (Duplicate all and Delete all when nothing is picked) centred on the list's column on a laptop; Delete happens at once, "8 lessons and 2 courses deleted" with Undo; a list emptied by Delete all shows the first-run empty state, never "No results for “”". `LearnYours`, `LearnYoursLaptop` (Apple Photos' Select; edge-lines: one edge for the boxes).

- **Edit's bar is Done and Publish (owner, 2026-11-17, the old edit bar circled: "why do we need this en ru switch. refactor this. and this menu and publish"):** both glass words, as every bar; EN | RU leaves the bar: the "Written in" property opens a menu at it (English ✓, Русский, Add a language), and the page shows the language being written. The "Written in" property, its menu and its Settings row are gone.

The same on a lesson, a course, a level and Courses, phone and laptop. ## Practice
- **The libraries' Edit as their boards, phone and laptop (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-19):** Edit was a page of its own in the app; it now arranges in place as the boards draw it: every shelf and row where it reads, a row's heart giving way to its grip, a shelf's ··· (Rename, Hide or Show) and grip at its head's end, over the rows' grips, a hidden shelf marked · Hidden, the search and place chips inert at half. On a laptop it is the same table: the shelf a head row of the table, each row an item row with its grip in the heart's column (`LibraryTable`'s `FoldHeadRow`, `ItemRow`, `TableFrame`, used by `ArrangeList`). Never a second list for Edit, or a row that moves on entering it.
- **The Practice libraries as their boards, and the boards' data the app's (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-19):** the Progressions board listed invented progressions; it reads the app's 57 from `library.ts` at build, in their nine styles, with the Key filter's counts from them. Show all n stood in the app as a soft pill under a shelf; it is the kit's quiet link (`ShowAll`, 15/600 in the link's ink with its ›), 8 under the card, in the Practice libraries and Songs alike, and the boards fold each shelf at five with it, as the app (owner 2026-11-16: a folded list's Show all is a quiet link under the card). On a phone a shelf's head is the board's 44 with its words 6 under the section's top and the card 8 under it, shelves 16 apart, a row's name 16/600 at 1.25 (it took 22 of line, in Songs' rows too); the board's cards were 12 round, against `DESIGN.md`'s 14, now 14. On a laptop the board's table had drifted: its rows stood 8 apart in a gapped column (64 a row, not the ruled 56); they stack flush, and the app's rows are 56 with the hairline inside, its columns at the board's shares worked out from the table's width (`shares`: Name 2 · Numerals 3 · Key .6; Accompaniment 2 · 3.4 · .8; Exercises 2 · 3 · 1), the header's words 13/600 8 over its line. The headings no longer sort: the Sort and filter sheet is the one way to sort, as the board (`SortHeading` gone). Measured to the pixel at 402 and 1710.
- **Edit progression as its board's form, and the board held to the one draft bar (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-19):** the app's editor was a document (the name as a large serif heading, how it sounds under it, Style as a dropdown row, the lines as two fields under a heading), centred on a laptop; the board (`PEEDIT`) is a form: Name (the kit's 52 name field), How it sounds (the 48 form field), Style as wrapping chips, Lines (ⓘ at the heading's end) as one card of two 48 rows, each key's numerals typed at the row's end in the serif, a line that does not read said under the card; sections 24 apart, 8 under the bar on a phone, 24 on a laptop, the column on the 56 edge at 704 with the bar naming the page (Edit progression, 30 bold). The board broke two of its own: a Draft pill and an explanation line hung under the bar, where every editor (the song's draft, a lesson) keeps the draft's acts in the bar (the glass ··· Publishing, the honey Publish, the gear), and The progression's serif card repeated the major line its Lines card holds; both go, and its minor line is the real one (i VI III VII). The draft's ··· is the glass circle on every editor (it was a bare icon), and a form field's words are 16 on a laptop too (`FormField`; they fell to 14). Measured to the pixel at 402 and 1710.
- **An editor that cannot open says why, as its boards' empty state (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-19):** a learner on Edit progression saw a grey lock, "Only an editor edits a progression." with its full stop and no line; the board (`PEGATE`, the kit's `EMPTY`) draws the honey tile with a pencil, the title without its stop, the way on ("Ask an editor, or use Make your own from it at the foot of the progression’s page.") and the honey Back to Progressions. Every editor's gate is now one `NotOpen` on the kit's `Said`, its reason's picture and line (an editor's pencil, an admin's lock, opening's spinner, offline's struck wifi, a failure's alert): Opening…, You’re offline (quiet, with the way back), It couldn’t be opened (Try again), en and ru; the score editor's own copy is gone. The gate stands where the boards' column starts, 8 under the bar on a phone and on its foot on a laptop, measured to the pixel at 402 and 1710.
- **New pattern as its boards, and the boards' figures the app's (loop, the board-to-app port, 2026-11-19):** the board offered twelve invented figures (Block chords, Alberti, Inner voice, Stride, Boogie…) where the app's editor offers its 45 right-hand and 27 left-hand figures under the same families; the board now lists the app's, by `RIGHT_FIGURE_GROUPS` and `LEFT_FIGURE_GROUPS`, and a new pattern opens as the app's does, unnamed on Whole-note chord over Root, whole note, its roll drawing them (it opened as a copy of Broken chords). In the app the form stands 8 under the bar on a phone (16), a family's name at normal line height, Cancel · Save each half the row in the quiet sand and the honey (Cancel was outlined, both their words' width); on a laptop the form keeps the sub-page's 56 edge (it was centred), stands 24 under the bar, the hands 16 under the band (32), and the roll's card has its "How it plays" caption over a roll drawn in the board's 280 units (taller for its width; `PatternRoll`'s `units`, en and ru).
